# Launch checklist

Kept by Lane F (the Desk). Docs-only commits, each pushed by itself on top of origin/main. Every row carries an owner,
a state and a proof. States: building, ready, pushing, live, checking, blocked, decision (Sam's call), unknown (asked,
not yet answered). "Proof" names where the evidence sits (a lane-notes section, a script, a screenshot folder); a
row with no proof is not done whatever its state says.

Last updated: 2026-10-19T04:15Z (Lane F; B2's e63 api-mode golden pass (rows 47 to 56) in the 04:20Z gap; Lane B's four diagnosis reads then warm at 04:35Z; Lane C's small push after e63 DONE).

Resolver fixes as proposed (carrera gt before the carrera nickname; 250 GT California keeps model 250 GT with trim California; Skyline GT-R keeps GT-R as the trim; IROC joins the trim vocabulary; the 2009 California year range corrected). 2. The failed-read fix, the most important: (a) a failed pool read retries ONCE under the request clock (opts.deadlineAt) before anything is shown; (b) if it still fails the card shows "Sam is still reading the sales for this one.", never "not tracked", never a range, never a count; (c) a failed read is NEVER stored as an answer and never written through refreshDemoAnswer or any stored-answer path; (d) "not tracked" only when the read succeeded and returned zero rows, with its fetch trace; (e) the read-only count task lists every car whose stored answer is not_tracked so genuine and failed ones are checked by name; then the quiet-gap read confirms the Ford GT. 4. The six cars become golden rows plus must-not-cross pairs (Ford GT vs Mustang GT, Porsche Carrera GT vs 911 Carrera, 250 GT California vs California models, Skyline GT-R vs Skyline), pinned by B2. 5. e64 ships after the thin-card push settles as the next engine bump with the usual after-reads, a cold column, then keep-warm warmDemo for the seven demo cars; the fix and guard live in oneEngineAnswer and the resolver, nothing in a page or card. 6. No throttle taken; Sam's decision.

- Lane B, 07:1xZ Oct 18: "(1) WITHDRAWN in lane-notes: the missing-index cause. The periodic CPU is not established, and I am not guessing. THE ONE PASTE (read-only, pg_stat_statements is enabled on Supabase projects by default and Sam used it earlier this week): select calls, round(total_exec_time::numeric) as total_ms, round(mean_exec_time::numeric, 1) as mean_ms, rows, left(query, 150) as query from pg_stat_statements where dbid = (select oid from pg_database where datname = current_database()) order by total_exec_time desc limit 10;  WHEN: run it twice, once at about :13 past the hour (just before a pull) and once at about :19 (just after the pull's three minutes), and compare the two grids: the statement whose total_ms GREW the most between them is what the pull episode spends its CPU on; the figures are cumulative since the last stats reset, so only the growth matters. WHAT EACH READING MEANS: a select on sales_archive with vin_norm in it whose mean_ms is in the hundreds or more = the planner is not using idx_sales_archive_vin_norm for that form (then I give an EXPLAIN); an insert or update on live_listings with 'on conflict' at the top = the pull's own upsert and status writes, which is index maintenance on live_listings (not the thirty on sales_archive, which the pull never rewrites); a select of vin_norm from sales_archive with 'in (' = liveStats' batches of eighty; a wide select from sales_archive with make, model and sale_date filters growing by thousands of rows = engine computes (the Tasks matching run at :05/:20/:35/:50 or a Market Check stale recompute) which are the known CPU cost per read; nothing user-visible growing while CPU is busy = autovacuum, confirmed by select * from pg_stat_progress_vacuum; and last_autovacuum in pg_stat_user_tables for sales_archive. If pg_stat_statements is not enabled the paste errors 'relation does not exist'; the next best read-only paste is select pid, now() - query_start as running_for, state, wait_event_type, left(query, 150) from pg_stat_activity where state <> 'idle' order by query_start; run at :16 during the pull. (2) FROM CODE, the pull's archive statements and their indexes: fillFinalPrices reads sales_archive where vin_norm = X and sale_date >= D order by sale_date desc limit 1 (equality on vin_norm: idx_sales_archive_vin_norm, then a sort of that car's few sales), and when that misses, where raw_record->>'url' = U limit 1 (the expression index sales_archive_raw_url_idx, same expression as PostgREST emits); liveStats reads sales_archive where vin_norm in (a list of 80) select vin_norm (the same btree, 80 probes) and auction_attempts where chassis_vin_norm in (80) (idx_attempts_vin). No function on vin_norm, no or= filter, no case-insensitive match, no large in-list; nothing in their form prevents index use, so the pull's lookups are not the CPU by construction, which is why the paste is needed. (3) THE PUSH, under Option A in the quiet gaps: the chain now waits for about four minutes past each quarter hour before the perf rows (retried once in the next gap if a run lands on an episode), the push, the warm, each after-read pass and the cold column. - Lane B, 05:5xZ Oct 18, the range's definition: "(1) WHAT THE RANGE IS: the low and high are the 25th and 75th percentiles of the included sales' prices, each rounded to the nearest $500 (lib/onebox.js r4Cluster), so the range is the middle half of the included sales, never a fence and never the lowest or highest sale. For the 2015 Porsche 911 GT3 on the stored e59 answer: 43 included sales, 21 inside $120,000 to $143,000, 11 below it (the lowest $83,000) and 11 above it (the highest $160,250; the $159,991 Sam saw is the second highest). So the heading as worded ('What these sell for ... based on 43 sales') does read as everything, and the truth is 'about half of the 43 sold inside it'. (2) THE LOWEST AND HIGHEST: the engine ALREADY carries them on the result tier as `span` = [lowest included sale rounded down to $500, highest rounded up] (r4Span over the included pool; the GT3's reads [83000, 160500]); it reaches Market Check and Sell as d.span and Buy and Tasks through coreOf (m.span when the kind is a range). It is null on the thin, single and none tiers by design (under eight sales there is no range, product rule 24), though the thin tier carries its own thin.span on the sales it shows. Adding the two as numeric siblings on every tier that has included sales (lowestSaleN, highestSaleN, unrounded) is an addition-only change in one place, every product sees it; I can put it in e60 before the chain runs if Sam wants it, or in the next bump. (3) 'USUAL RANGE, NOT THE HIGHEST OR LOWEST': true by construction for every car with a range: the band is the middle half, so by definition about a quarter of the included sales sit below it and a quarter above it, and the lowest and highest included sales are outside it whenever the pool has more than four distinct prices (the only exception is a pool where the lowest or highest price repeats enough to reach the quartile, a tie, when the band's edge equals that price). The exact true sentence for every car is 'the middle half of the included sales landed between X and Y; about a quarter sold for less and a quarter for more', with the lowest and highest sale shown beside it from span."

- Lane B, 04:4xZ Oct 18, run-0 v2: "the run-0 bug is fixed and the run files are re-checked; no database touched. (1) THE BUG: Postgres has no max() aggregate for uuid, so the batch boundary is now the batch's last id taken in descending order (select the batch in id order, limit batch_rows, then its last row), exact, and the loop exits when the select returns no row. Fixed in run-0 v2, run-2 and run-2b (the three places it lived; run-1, run-4 and run-5 never had it). (2) THE RE-CHECK against Postgres 15 and 17 (Supabase's versions; I cannot read version() myself, so PART 1 of v2 is `select version();` for Sam to send back): clock_timestamp, extract(epoch from interval), get diagnostics row_count, pg_total_relation_size, pg_size_pretty, count(*) filter, tablesample system, rollup, create or replace procedure with COMMIT (PG11+) all exist on both. Two editor facts changed the files: (a) the Supabase editor does NOT show RAISE NOTICE output, so every per-batch timing is now a ROW in a small RLS-locked log table (public.sales_archive_fill_log, created in the same paste with enable RLS and revoke lines per the standing rule) and Sam reads it with a select; (b) a paste with several statements runs as one implicit transaction, so a CALL that COMMITs must be the ONLY statement in its paste, and VACUUM (run-5) stays one statement alone. (3) THE FILE: ~/Desktop/goasksam-proofs/db/run-0-scratch-proof-v2.sql, split into numbered parts, each pasted alone: PART 1 version; PART 2 setup (scratch copy of 20,000 rows, the ten columns, the log tables, two scratch procedures); PART 3 the fill, one CALL line (four batches of 5,000); PART 4 the timings grid and the progress row; PART 5 the verification grid (every _diff must be 0); PART 6 the size grid (scratch total and heap, and the real table's size now); PART 3b the COMMIT question (reset, then the COMMIT variant alone: success means run-2 is the real procedure, 'invalid transaction termination' means run-2b); PART 7 clean-up, which is also the rollback. Sam sends back the grids of parts 1, 4, 5, 6 and the outcome of 3b. The v1 file is renamed superseded-... so it cannot be run by mistake. run-3's text now reads the log table instead of notices. (4) Lane C's correction accepted with thanks: lib/onebox.js line 4861 does return setAsideRows with a reason per row on the result tier (I had read shapeCards alone); no e60 addition is needed and my earlier line to Lane C is withdrawn on that point. waits are reported in the consolidated report, not here.
